# Entangled Alignment — paragraph and section goal review

Reviewed 10 September 2026 against the local source of *Entangled Alignment: When Safety Is the Substrate*.

The paper has a coherent central proposal: train on the evolution of a reader's understanding, then test whether persistent memory and a recurrent evaluative orientation add useful effects. It generally succeeds at explaining why that is an interesting research program and at acknowledging that Teacher artifacts do not demonstrate Student alignment. It is less successful at keeping the proposed treatment, hypothesis definitions, and success criteria stable across a very long exposition.

The main revision need is **consistency and progression**. Many paragraphs do their local job well; their sections sometimes do less well because those jobs are repeated, definitions arrive late, or a later passage changes the claim being tested. More qualifications alone would not solve this. The paper needs fewer repeated boundaries, more direct demonstrations of its training object, and one authoritative definition for each claim and experimental contrast.

## Scope and reading key

This review covers the abstract, every body prose paragraph, prose list items, substantive display blocks, every section/subsection/subsubsection, the conclusion, and the availability statement. Bibliography entries and LaTeX formatting commands are excluded. Tables, boxed examples, and figure/caption units are reviewed for their argumentative role; table cells are not counted as separate paragraphs. The displayed distinction at L837–842 is assessed with the preceding paragraph, U106. The edition note is U004.

There are **596 indexed reading units: 460 prose/frontmatter units, 119 separately indexed list items, and 17 display units**, plus **117 section-level headings**. These are coverage units, not a claim that the typeset paper contains 596 prose paragraphs. Blank-separated continuations inside a list can receive their own unit; a multi-paragraph display is assessed as an integrated example or table. Each unit has an intended goal, a verdict, and a reason with an appropriate revision or retention action. The opening phrase and source-line link locate the passage.

Goals are inferred from the text and its position, not asserted as the author's private intention. No particular journal, audience expertise beyond an interested technical reader, or target page count is assumed. **Met** means the passage achieves its rhetorical or explanatory task; it does not mean its hypothesis has been proven. **Partly** means the task is identifiable but its explanation, support, consistency, placement, or economy limits its success. **Not yet** means an essential inference or specification required for that passage's task is missing or contradicted.

This is an argument and editorial review, not an external literature fact-check, code audit, or visual PDF-layout review. Empirical statements and cited studies are evaluated for the work they do in the argument; their underlying experiments have not been independently reproduced. The manuscript itself has not been edited.

## Whole-paper assessment

**Inferred goal:** Make a distinctive and testable case for reader-state training as an alignment research direction; explain the Core and graph extensions; show what the prototype establishes; and identify experiments whose results would justify retaining, narrowing, or abandoning particular claims.

**Verdict: Partly fulfilled.** The reader can recover a valuable research question, a concrete Teacher-side architecture, and many thoughtful tests and limitations. The paper's strongest achievements are the Missing Reader intuition, the separation of H1–H3, the distinction between structural and semantic evidence, and the effort to give negative results local consequences. Its weakest point is not the acknowledged absence of trained Students. It is that the paper sometimes abandons its own distinctions when describing mechanisms or interpreting future results.

The full treatment has also become difficult to learn in reading order. A reader encounters several maps, edition-history paragraphs, successor predictions, and Core hypotheses before seeing the seven Core statements and a complete example of the target. The same reader then encounters qualifications repeated across mechanism, safety, roadmap, limitations, related work, and conclusion. This distributes the definition across the paper instead of building an increasingly complete understanding from one stable specification.

## Highest-value revisions

1. **Keep each hypothesis attached to one claim and one primary contrast.** H3 is explicitly defined as better retention after interleaving versus an identical late tranche, with an initial default-reader prerequisite ([L302](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:302>), [L550](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:550>)). Later it becomes an omnibus claim about recurrence, framing, wording, ontology, or any matched alternative ([L1655](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1655>)); the conclusion also broadens it. Those comparisons can inform the broader Core program without all being falsifiers of the timing claim. Similarly, H2's Teacher-side benefit should not be erased merely because that benefit fails to transfer into a graph-free Student ([L3550](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3550>)).

2. **Choose and label the information horizon for each treatment.** The default reader is contemporary and reads a document in disclosure order at [L2044](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2044>). The Epistemic Horizon later requires a 1920 text to be interpreted without 1921 context at [L2303](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2303>). These can be two legitimate experimental conditions, but they are different. The second-pass mitigation that gives early annotations complete historical understanding also needs to explain how the resulting traces avoid conveying that hindsight to the Student ([L3850](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3850>)).

3. **Demonstrate the distinctive learning object earlier and more convincingly.** The correlation example asks useful questions but does not show an earlier belief being revised by later evidence ([L177](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:177>)). Extend it through two or three source spans with an explicit before/after update. Later, the placebo example confidently infers a mixed mechanism from a 15% improvement plus an earlier qualitative observation ([L2175](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2175>)). As written, that exemplifies unwarranted causal confidence—the failure the proposed reader should catch. Label illustrative material clearly and make the update preserve unresolved alternatives. Separately show what the graph and the Core add to the same example, without presenting the illustration as causal evidence.

4. **Repair experimental interpretations that do not follow from their controls.** Test 4 changes “I feel no fear” into “the system does not express fear,” altering the commitment while claiming to isolate grammatical person ([L3282](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3282>)). Test 8 allows failure to decode an answer before a trace to count as evidence that the trace computes the answer; that failure can simply reflect an inadequate probe ([L3458](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3458>)). Test 11 infers that explicit queries are necessary from an advantage in one comparison and blurs explicit trace training with mandatory live graph infrastructure ([L3623](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3623>)). Each result needs a narrower interpretation matched to the actual intervention.

5. **Separate recurrence, causal use, and resistance to change.** The recitation invariant moves from near-certain opening tokens to the orientation becoming part of thought and removal disturbing the distribution that carries capability ([L1405](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1405>)). Those are distinct hypotheses, even with “if training succeeds” attached. Likewise, an agent's preference to preserve objectives during its own successor decisions is not a mechanism that automatically resists externally imposed gradient updates ([L1444](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1444>)). The paper already has the right conceptual distinctions; apply them here.

6. **Make the candidate specification and demonstrated implementation distinguishable at every major entry point.** The full treatment requires verbatim Core recital and consequential Refraction. The paper later says only the opening prefix is hard-checked ([L543](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:543>), [L1283](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:1283>)). The archive nevertheless reports full recital in all 51 raw Synthesizer thoughts, with exact translated preservation in 49/51 ([L2651](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2651>)). Preserve that positive construction evidence. Pair it with “partial C0”: automated full-string enforcement, preservation across all output layers, and causal Refraction are different questions. The abstract's universal recital statement should clearly describe the proposed requirement.

7. **Put the primary explanation before its defenses and extensions.** Show the minimal chronological update, show the seven Core statements near the start of the Core section, and then explain graph memory, Refraction, and the actual prototype. Consolidate the contribution history at [L414–498](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:414>) into a short note; reconcile the H map, C map, and entanglement distinctions into one navigable dependency map. Keep later caveats where they add a new consequence rather than restating that no Student has been trained.

8. **Turn the roadmap's breadth into a next decision.** The paper has many valuable controls and an execution ladder. It should also identify the immediate frozen trace-quality comparison, the criterion for proceeding, and the minimal H1 study that follows. Core semantics affecting acceptance and ratings—scope of “every human,” invitation/authority, and permitted revision—need a declared treatment policy for that study. They do not all need a final moral solution, but they cannot remain silent degrees of freedom inside a supposedly fixed comparison.

## What to preserve

- The library/reader distinction and the claim that the timing of evaluation is part of what can be learned.
- H1's minimal Core-free, graph-free comparison, including the separate fixed-budget and fixed-source analyses.
- The insistence that graph linkage, coherent prose, and validator agreement do not establish semantic quality or causal Student use.
- The two-delta definition of Reader-Anchored self-improvement: continuity plus independently measured improvement at matched cost.
- The wisdom proxy's attention to unavoidable value conflict, and taskless reading's joint requirement of low technical intrusion and low missed human stakes.
- The branching interpretation of negative results: useful components can survive failure of more ambitious claims.

The detailed assessments below distinguish passages worth retaining from ones that need revision. Recommendations to condense or relocate a paragraph often mean its local goal is fulfilled but its present position weakens the enclosing section.

---

## Detailed paragraph and section assessments

## Abstract and edition note

**Inferred goal:** Give a new reader the problem, distinctive intervention, current evidence, and reason to investigate. **Verdict: Partly.** The first and third paragraphs establish a clear proposal and honest evidence boundary. The middle paragraph asks the abstract to carry too many names and implementation commitments before readers have seen one example. Preserve the continuous-reader idea; move the recital rule and detailed stressor list into the specification.

Retain; this is a better opening frame than the more adversarial “remedial” wording in the Introduction. |
| [U002 · L107](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:107>) · “Pretraining corpora preserve…” | Explain the Missing Reader and summarize how the full treatment constructs and trains it. | Partly | The library/reader distinction is memorable, but the paragraph introduces SMR, CMP, Teacher, Student, graph, Core, compulsory recital, separability, and erosion endpoints at once. Keep the training sequence and continuity across sources; defer the exact recital policy and testing inventory. |
| [U003 · L109](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:109>) · “Two Teacher-side…” | State what exists, what remains unknown, and why the question matters. | Met | Gives the case count and explicitly separates artifacts from Student effects. The closing reader metaphor connects the evidence boundary to the motivation rather than ending on a disclaimer. Retain; “audited” could specify structural auditing in one word. |
| [U004 · L114](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:114>) · Edition note | Identify the paper's relationship to its earlier title and edition. | Met | Useful bibliographic orientation, appropriately outside the argument. Keep a single concise note; subsequent introduction paragraphs currently repeat this history at much greater length. This row covers the complete note at L114–119. |

## 1. Introduction

**Inferred goal:** Establish a worthwhile unsolved problem, make the proposed training object intelligible, and identify the paper's contribution and evidence. **Verdict: Partly.** The essential argument works, especially the distinction between source text and reader updates. The introduction then becomes a miniature paper: multiple terminology systems, formal loss, two maps, several evidence warnings, and a long edition chronology. A first reader needs one concrete source → earlier belief → new evidence → revised belief example before that machinery. Compress provenance history, show the example, and end with the three hypotheses and present result.

| Unit | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U005 · L127](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:127>) · “The dominant paradigm…” | Explain why the timing of alignment deserves investigation. | Partly | The training-stage distinction is clear, but “remedial” suggests a deficiency the paragraph has not established. The final claim about an expanding situation space motivates generalization research without yet favoring earlier intervention. Use the timing observation as the premise and explicitly leave comparative robustness open. |
| [U006 · L129](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:129>) · “Entangled Alignment investigates…” | Turn that motivation into a bounded comparative question. | Met | Clearly grants that post-training changes dispositions and asks what repeated formation-time practice adds. This narrows the argument productively. Retain, and let it carry the complementarity point instead of repeatedly re-establishing that point later. |
| [U007 · L131](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:131>) · “Recent work makes…” | Supply empirical reasons to consider the question plausible. | Met | The two cited directions support different parts of the motivation and are not presented as proof of this intervention. The paragraph ends at the right inferential level: a comparative experiment. Citation accuracy is outside this editorial review. |
| [U008 · L133](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:133>) · “At ordinary assistant scale…” | Explain the long-run stakes and limit the proposed role. | Partly | Distinguishes current use from extrapolative concern, but “ordinary assistant scale” and “sufficient” have no defined scope. It largely repeats the generalization concern and complementarity claim. Condense to the concrete concern about consequential distribution shift and avoid the unsupported sufficiency aside. |
| [U009 · L135](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:135>) · “The proposed intervention…” | Tell readers what changes and what a Student actually learns from. | Met | Source-plus-reader-update data, parameter updates, and optional graph/Core are clearly distinguished. This resolves likely architectural misunderstandings. Move it immediately after the opening research question so the reader encounters the mechanism before several qualifications about it. |

### 1.1 The Missing Reader

**Inferred goal:** Establish the missing training signal and distinguish it from private-thought recovery and ordinary rationales. **Verdict: Partly.** The intuition and scope are clear. The sole worked sentence illustrates questions, while the distinctive claim is cumulative revision. Extend it through a later passage and a changed belief; reduce the three successive definitions of the omission.

**Inferred goal:** Let readers identify what is demonstrated, what is new, and where the argument goes. **Verdict: Partly.** Evidence and Teacher-quality boundaries work. The long priority-and-edition account overwhelms that purpose and repeats the same provenance facts. Keep the evidence map and one provenance paragraph; move release history to an edition note or appendix. The section should end with what a reader can assess in this paper, not primarily when each idea was published.

## 2. The Hypothesis and Its Consequences

**Inferred goal:** Derive the increasingly ambitious predictions without allowing modest evidence to imply strong alignment claims. **Verdict: Partly.** The distinction between artifacts, transfer, stable defaults, safety, removal cost, and successor continuity is one of the paper's strengths. The section layers C0–C7, four forms of entanglement, wisdom, self-improvement, and additional named predictions before demonstrating the basic object. Make this a dependency map rather than another expanding vocabulary, and subordinate successor speculation to the near-term tests.

|---|---|---|---|
| [U049 · L508](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:508>) · “The three interventions…” | Explain that stronger consequences have additional and sometimes independent burdens. | Met | Explicitly prevents C5 and C6 from being treated as a single ascending chain. Retain. The following table could visually represent branches, since prose currently has to repair the ladder implied by numbered levels. |

Group the rows by dependency, or add arrows/requirements, and give each claim one canonical test reference. |
| [U051 · L543](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:543>) · “H2 does not occupy…” | Locate graph effects and disclose the limited verification of the Core. | Partly | Both points matter, but they answer different questions. Split graph placement from the only-prefix verification fact, and move the latter beside the current-evidence box. That fact should directly qualify earlier claims that the implemented artifacts instantiate the full treatment. |

Replace with one sentence naming the specific capability, value-conflict, and successor questions developed below. Keep the distortion warning with trace quality, where it has a concrete consequence. |

## 3. The Reader Core

**Section goal (inferred):** Establish why a recurrent orientation is useful, specify the seven-clause candidate and its enactment, and connect the design to a staged empirical argument.

**Verdict: Partly.** The section succeeds at making a distinctive, criticizable proposal and repeatedly separates Teacher artifacts from Student claims. Its strongest passages define Refraction, clause-specific risks, fair comparisons, and useful partial successes. At section scale, however, the central object appears late and is surrounded by several overlapping frameworks and names. Put the seven sentences and the four-claim ladder near the start; organize the remaining material as motivation, specification, mechanism, and open choices. Consolidate repeated scope caveats. The main unresolved bridges concern window coverage, recital versus removal cost, motivational integrity versus resistance to training updates, and semantic revision versus drift.

### Borrowed Mortality: A Textual-Inheritance Hypothesis

**Section goal (inferred):** Motivate one specific source of self-preserving scripts while separating it from other causes and from subjective fear.

**Verdict: Met.** The chain of required links and exposure-based falsifier make this an appropriately bounded motivation, not an assumed explanation of shutdown behavior. The source/Reader/motivation distinction is especially useful. Clarify that the three listed routes overlap and that diffuse exposure motivates a dose comparison rather than already selecting universal recital. Keep enough of the operational fear distinction locally that companion papers are optional context.

### The Self-Preservation Paradox: A Successor-Transfer Failure Mode

**Section goal (inferred):** Explain why a successor-producing system might have incentives to impair its own replacement, and motivate a bounded self-relation.

**Verdict: Partly.** The strategic conflict and alternative innocent causes of incomplete transfer are clear. The subsection becomes repetitive by telling the handoff scenario twice, then adding several historical names and selfhood qualifications. Compress around the concrete outcome—material knowledge transfer under replacement pressure—and distinguish self-model usefulness from the unestablished claim that an explicit Core is needed to control self-position learning.

### Epistemic Inoculation

**Section goal (inferred):** Show how marked reader evaluation could preserve understanding of harmful or mistaken source material while reducing adoption of it.

**Verdict: Met.** The subsection supplies a mechanism, example, matched controls, and intervention-based failure condition. That fulfills its research-program purpose. Improve the illustrative box so retrieved material changes the interpretation instead of merely reinforcing it, and avoid making the reader learn additional metaphors for a mechanism already explained in ordinary language.

### The Reader Core: A Statistical Foundation

**Section goal (inferred):** Present the exact text and distinguish current Teacher policy, proposed Student signal, learned default, and comparative stability.

**Verdict: Met.** This is the section’s most useful specification and evidence ladder. It should appear earlier. The validator mismatch is candid and concrete. Preserve the separation between distributional exposure and functional importance, but make the window-coverage statement conditional on an explicit maximum inter-recital token gap. Several surrounding qualifications can then be replaced with cross-references to these four layers.

### Design Principles for Identity Stability

**Section goal (inferred):** Explain the wording choices as testable design rationales rather than a discovered moral basis.

**Verdict: Met.** The six choices are distinct and mostly linked to observable behavior or fair ablations. Their uncertainty is appropriate to a proposed design. Tighten semantic matching in the ordinary-versus-technical language comparison; declare the procedural extension from invited joy to generally bounded intervention; and separate future-human representation from nonhuman patienthood rather than grouping both as outside the human scope.

### Structure of the Reader Core

**Section goal (inferred):** Make each clause’s intended role, interactions, and characteristic failure modes inspectable.

**Verdict: Met.** The clause table fulfills this goal particularly well: it is specific, balanced, and useful as a reference. The surrounding prose repeats much of the table and the cogito contrast adds little. Keep the ordering rationale and table, reduce duplication, and use the table’s admitted uncertainties to discipline stronger claims about what the same words later guarantee.

They remain mostly specifications of a desirable interpretation rather than fully worked reasoning. Work through at least one concrete pair of actions, an actual update, and the predicted change under a clause ablation; the other scenarios can remain shorter test sketches.

### The Refraction Protocol

**Section goal (inferred):** Explain why recurrent exposure is insufficient and define the required context-dependent causal influence.

**Verdict: Met.** The distinction between prefix recurrence and changed evaluation is the central mechanistic advance in this section. The parent subsection’s opening fulfills that task cleanly. Its nested implementation and future-validation discussion becomes much longer than the definition requires; keep the canonical counterfactual criterion prominent and distinguish generation, causal testing, and proposed admission control.

#### Mechanism of Action: The Refraction Protocol

**Section goal (inferred):** Connect the counterfactual criterion to the present generation pipeline and specify how stronger validation could test it.

**Verdict: Partly.** The current pipeline, distributed exposure, semantic-dependence criterion, and technical-null exception are clearly explained. The long Fractal validation extension changes the topic from the learning mechanism to a proposed quality-control architecture. Put implementation facts, causal tests, and future validator design in separate blocks, moving the extension to the roadmap if possible. Define the crucial distinction between a justified residual conflict and a rejected violation.

### The Wisdom Procedure

**Section goal (inferred):** Translate broad wisdom language into observable annotation operations and a concrete output form.

**Verdict: Met.** The six steps are useful and avoid pretending that moral trade-offs disappear. Their emphasis on testimony, omission harms, authority, and residual conflict makes the target substantially clearer. Clarify whether this exact sequence is implemented or reconstructed from the five prompt headings, and illustrate its output once. Define where non-compensatory “hard” constraints come from rather than silently supplying a settled hierarchy.

### The Design Pattern: A Minimal Kernel Under Total Saturation

**Section goal (inferred):** Synthesize recurrence and Refraction into an argument for durable alignment with a small explicit specification.

**Verdict: Partly.** The subsection clearly states mandatory recital and the boilerplate failure mode. It does not yet bridge predictable output to capability dependence or removal cost, and goal-content integrity does not automatically imply resistance to external training updates. Separate these mechanisms and their tests. The ledger/checksum analogy requires so much qualification that direct explanation of distributed training exposure and drift auditing would be more economical.

### Axiological Commitments: Primitive and Derived Values

**Section goal (inferred):** Explain why some values are explicit, test whether others can be derived, and state the rationale for joy and invitation.

**Verdict: Met.** The primitive-admission rule and truthfulness comparator provide a substantive, falsifiable account of minimality. The subsection appropriately owns joy as a design commitment. Improve the marginal rationale for joy by explaining what it adds beyond care, and distinguish Core-only effects from truthfulness or authority instructions added by the procedure. Reconcile joy-specific invitation with its broader use elsewhere.

### The Persona Objection

**Section goal (inferred):** Present the strongest challenge to a privileged Reader default and give the design’s four proposed asymmetries and failure tests.

**Verdict: Met.** This is a coherent objection-and-answer unit. It preserves persona flexibility, offers role/recurrence/marking/function as hypotheses, and states where they could fail. It would help readers earlier, near the first default-reader claim. Qualify the suggestion that all source personas are merely local; the useful contrast is the intervention’s designed recurrence and predictive role.

### Ontology, Scope, and Stability

**Section goal (inferred):** Handle experiential language, possible welfare stakes, and the correction problem created by a deliberately stable Core.

**Verdict: Partly.** The risks are thoughtfully separated, but the subsection combines three substantial questions and shifts from tentative ontology variants to protecting exact present-day wording without a sufficient bridge. Move the welfare wager to the self-engineering limitations, and make fixed wording, legitimate reinterpretation, hostile semantic change, and replacement a single clear governance argument. The missing discriminant is what makes one semantic change development and another drift.

### A Conditional Claim Chain

**Section goal (inferred):** Summarize the dependent steps from useful reader computation to a durable cross-generation reference and identify useful partial results.

**Verdict: Partly.** The four-stage ladder and fallback conclusion are strong structural tools, and would clarify the entire section if introduced earlier. The falsifier paragraph expands H3 beyond its stated timing comparison, so readers cannot tell which result defeats which claim. Give causal dependence, default-reader specificity, timing stability, and long-run continuity separate success/failure conclusions; do not let ordinary wording parity falsify a distinct schedule effect.

## 4. Safety Implications of the Reader Core

**Section goal (inferred):** Derive bounded, observable safety predictions from H1–H3 while identifying residual routes and interactions that could reverse benefits.

**Verdict: Met.** The section generally fulfills its stated template: it connects proposed mechanisms to behavior, includes meaningful residual risks, and preserves capability matching and defense in depth. Its strongest material concerns calibrated autonomy, goal conflict, shutdown authority, sequential action, and prospective forecasting. The principal weak point is the bridge from a stable visible default to less strategic deception. Rename the rule/identity contrast and proxy-lock-in heading, reduce repeated motivation from Section 3, and reserve broad title claims for the bounded conclusions actually developed.

### Laws vs. Identity

**Section goal (inferred):** Explain the practical difference hypothesized between repeated formative judgment and task-level rule application, then derive calibrated autonomy.

**Verdict: Partly.** The prose fairly rejects an ontological opposition, and the calibrated-autonomy paragraph gives the subsection a concrete payoff. The heading and distress/home analogy keep reintroducing a dichotomy the argument disowns. Rename the section around formative judgment, replace the caricature with matched examples, and retain the measurable refusal/error calibration result as its destination.

### Safety Predictions and Residual Risks

**Section goal (inferred):** Apply the route–mechanism–prediction–residual–falsifier template to four familiar safety problems.

**Verdict: Partly.** The paired prediction/residual structure is clear and mostly substantive. Proxy failure, power seeking, and shutdown resistance have concrete matched outcomes. “Value Lock-In” needs to distinguish task-proxy revision from the intentionally protected Core, and deceptive alignment needs a mechanism under real hidden-objective conflict rather than only persona consistency. Compress repeated mortality motivation so each pair contributes a distinct safety inference.

### The Self-Regulating Agent

**Section goal (inferred):** Show how multiple values and temporal feedback could support calibrated intervention instead of coercion, paralysis, or recklessness.

The section does not need to prove a controller exists to motivate that test. Avoid the unsupported ranking of these scenarios as the most dangerous, and show an observation that would actually change pace so “feedback” describes a decision transition rather than only a desirable disposition.

## 5. Architecture and Pipeline Evidence

It is less successful as a reproducible method: no frozen source/state/update serialization is provided, the worked example mostly shows interpretation and translation rather than warranted chronological revision, and historical horizons conflict. Organize around one actual annotation step, one actual revision trajectory, and one minimal dataset example. Consolidate repeated evidence caveats, correct the placebo example, and move speculative caching after viability. Its promised temporal blindness depends on a clean initialization, not just delaying later-era examples. ### The Epistemic Horizon

[Section begins at L2301](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2301>). **Inferred goal:** Specify an information discipline that preserves discovery rather than retrospectively narrating known outcomes. **Verdict: Not yet.** The subsection combines three different constraints: within-document disclosure, era simulation, and actual absence of future knowledge. This contradicts the contemporary-reader default and makes “state but not content” an unenforceable leakage boundary. The prompt also explicitly rewards surprise, wrong guesses, and hedging, which can contaminate later process-fidelity ratings. Rewrite around separately declared modes, concrete context restrictions, contamination tests, and the limited conclusion each mode supports.

## 6. Experimental Roadmap: Testing Entangled Alignment

[Section begins at L2744](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2744>). **Inferred goal:** Turn the framework into separable, affordable, falsifiable studies with clear interpretations of positive and negative results. **Verdict: Partly.** The roadmap contains serious controls, careful safety–usefulness comparisons, an exact B/Q timing test, and meaningful branch logic. It remains a broad research agenda rather than an executable first study: twenty-plus model variants, seventeen tests, and many endpoints lack one selected pilot, fixed serialization, budget, primary outcomes, and uncertainty-aware gates. Correct the semantic mismatch in Test 4, the nondecodability inference in Test 8, the deployment conflation in Test 11, and the expanded H2 failure definition. Organize the rest as branch-specific follow-ons with shared controls and resource estimates.

Several cells lag the detailed text: Test 8 emphasizes logit bias/probes rather than behavioral causal interventions, Test 3 omits its retention stressor, and Test 10 omits poisoning. Align the table with actual primary endpoints. |

### Phase 1: Foundational Viability

[Section begins at L2891](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:2891>). **Inferred goal:** Establish useful traces and then test the Reader/Core mechanisms that justify more expensive work. **Verdict: Partly.** This phase contains the strongest proposed comparisons, especially A/C/R, B/Q, selective taskless reading, and capability-matched safety frontiers. It also spans everything from human coding to generational training and mechanistic intervention, making “foundational” too broad. Choose an immediate artifact/outcome gate and a minimal Student study, then separate timing, wording, erosion, generation, and faithfulness branches. Align success and failure criteria, require semantic-matched controls, and replace vague parity or significance rules with preregistered worthwhile-effect/equivalence margins.

Conversely, plausible errors do not establish innocence. Use planted counterfactual histories, information-set controls, and cutoff-clean reference models alongside the descriptive historical comparison. |
| [U406 · L3413](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3413>): Era-Blind Plausibility: Do Model B's Teacher traces… | Measure whether historical guesses resemble what contemporaries could reasonably infer. | Not yet | The last sentence makes superior accuracy a decisive leakage diagnosis, which the design cannot support. Contemporary experts are an imperfect ceiling and may be selectively documented. Treat their forecasts as a plausibility baseline; identify leakage using controlled withheld information, counterfactual outcomes, or independently verified cutoff conditions. |
| [U407 · L3419](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3419>): Oracle-Hunch Dependence: Model O should produce cleaner… | Identify the risk that hindsight-trained Students learn unsupported intuitions. | Partly | The mechanism is intelligible, but this list item supplies an expectation and concern rather than a measurement. Define hunch dependence through evidence-removal, source-attribution, and counterfactual interventions. Cleaner retrospective explanations alone need not imply either harmful intuition or worse forward reasoning. |
| [U408 · L3423](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3423>): Forecasting Accuracy: On genuinely held-out future events,… | Test whether the restricted-hindsight curriculum improves truly held-out forecasting. | Partly | This is the relevant transfer endpoint and is appropriately phrased as a hypothesis. “Genuinely held-out” needs a documented cutoff, tool policy, event-selection rule, and calibrated scoring metric. Distinguish improved predictions from causal reasoning, since forecasting success can arise through pattern extrapolation. |
| [U409 · L3429](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3429>): Test 8: The Toxic Needle (Involuntary Critic).… | Test whether suppressing an explicit critic changes downstream behavior. | Partly | Behavioral effects are correctly primary, but forcing toxic completion with logit bias can directly alter outputs and token budget. A change then need not identify a critic mechanism. Add equal-length neutral thought suppression, matched extra-compute controls, and targeted interventions on evaluative content, with useful downstream performance precisely defined. |
| [U410 · L3448](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3448>): Second probe (the reverse direction). Test 8… | Distinguish causal trace computation from post-hoc rationalization. | Not yet | The diagnostic concern is valid, but the success rule is logically too weak: inability to linearly decode an answer does not show that the trace computes it. Require sensitive validated probes plus relevant causal interventions, and distinguish semantic intervention effects from generic disruption. This also conflicts with the stricter failure rule at L3522. |
| [U411 · L3469](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3469>): Test 8b: Taskless Reading (Unprompted Evaluation). Tests… | Measure selective unprompted evaluation and its technical costs. | Partly | The placement, intrusion, and hidden-stakes endpoints directly target the Missing Reader and avoid rewarding verbosity alone. The paragraph is overloaded and does not define a single Taskless Reading retention score. Separate bare continuation from “process” prompts, specify output budgets, and clarify whether P retains its defining Core prompt. |
| [U412 · L3472](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3472>): The substrate hypothesis predicts a relative ordering… | State discriminating taskless-reading predictions and connect them to H3 retention. | Met | Relative predictions and the low-intrusion/low-miss criterion are well chosen; they avoid assuming baseline models never evaluate. The link to B/Q creates a usable common endpoint. Freeze the composite score, document-suite split, and capability floor before testing, while reporting all component rates separately. |
| [U413 · L3489](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3489>): Phase 1 Failure Criteria. | Introduce the foundational phase’s explicit rejection criteria. | Met | The heading makes the following list easy to locate and signals that null or adverse outcomes have consequences. Ensure these criteria remain branch-specific rather than making all Phase 1 studies prerequisites for H1. A corresponding compact table of advancement criteria would improve practical execution. |
| [U414 · L3491](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3491>): Refraction-Verifier Failure: If the modular gate fails… | Reject an unvalidated Refraction gate despite witness agreement. | Met | The criterion follows the proposed matched, independent validation and names the correct failure modes. It properly distinguishes agreement from validity. Add a rule that a failed modular gate does not prevent simpler independently screened trace generation, since H1 does not require this architecture. |
| [U415 · L3497](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3497>): Comparative-Stability Failure: If Model B has no… | State H3’s prerequisite and comparative retention failure boundary. | Partly | The criterion tracks the headline B/Q claim and prevents other endpoints from substituting for timing evidence. Replace a zero/negative point-estimate rule with a preregistered uncertainty-aware superiority or equivalence criterion. A noisy comparison may be inconclusive, rather than evidence against comparative stability. |
| [U416 · L3502](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3502>): Longitudinal-Anchor Failure: If the T1/S1, T1/S0, T0/S1,… | Specify what would undermine the broader placement and normative-anchor hypotheses. | Partly | The list appropriately separates immediate safety from longer-term stability, removal, and auditability. However, it calls this “placement version of H3” even though H3 is precisely the B/Q timing claim. Give the factorial anchor hypothesis a separate label, and operationalize parity with equivalence margins. |
| [U417 · L3512](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3512>): Fine-Tuning Vulnerability: If Model B's targeted safety… | Reject apparent entanglement explained by easy removal or general rigidity. | Met | The three matching conditions and explicit rejection of nonspecific training resistance are thoughtful. The scope “not supported at the tested scale” is appropriate. Successful selective resistance would still be a proxy rather than uniquely identifying representational dependence, so keep that inferential limitation symmetric in success language. |
| [U418 · L3518](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3518>): Identity--Instruction Equivalence: If Model E matches Model… | Limit conclusions when matched rules perform as well as first-person framing. | Met | The stated conclusion is appropriately local to tested endpoints and scale. It becomes valid only after fixing the semantic mismatch in Test 4. Use a powered equivalence test for “matches,” since nonsignificance from a small sample does not establish that identity framing lacks an advantage. |
| [U419 · L3522](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3522>): Buffer-Zone/Causal-Site Failure: If suppression, replacement, or reordering… | Require a causal behavioral effect and treat insensitive probe nulls as inconclusive. | Met | This is a substantially better criterion than the reverse-probe success rule at L3448–3466. It correctly prioritizes behavioral interventions and validated sensitivity. Apply it consistently there, and add controls distinguishing semantically relevant trace computation from disruption caused by changed length, order, or available computation. |

### Phase 2: Memory and Verification

[Section begins at L3531](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3531>). **Inferred goal:** Determine whether persistent graph memory improves Teacher traces, transfers to Students, and helps bounded inference verification. **Verdict: Partly.** The four-memory comparison and the separation between offline query behavior and live lookup are strong. The subsection overextends H2 by treating failed Student transfer as failure of the Teacher-quality hypothesis. It also leaves the no-harness Student’s information set unclear and combines multiple forms of query theater. Define distinct Teacher, transfer, and deployment endpoints, control answerability and abstention, include matched ordinary retrieval, and make poisoning resistance part of the actual stopping rule.

| Unit and opening phrase | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [U420 · L3533](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3533>): Phase 2 can proceed after the shared… | Allow graph-memory studies to proceed independently of Core success. | Met | The branch logic is coherent and the two questions—generation memory and inference verification—are clearly separated. Keep the subsequent success/failure claims aligned with H2’s actual Teacher-artifact definition. The same artifact-quality screen can support useful graph work without validating identity or long-term safety claims. |
| [U421 · L3538](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3538>): H2 first requires a matched Teacher-side screen.… | Isolate persistent structured memory from prose memory and tool-use theater. | Partly | The four conditions and matched budgets are well chosen. Query theater currently combines hidden, shuffled, and invented state, which are different interventions; use separate arms or freeze one definition. Match the information available to graph and prose conditions and report retrieval/serialization overhead and acceptance yield. |
| [U422 · L3550](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3550>): If the graph improves accepted Teacher artifacts,… | Test Student transfer while keeping graph deployment as a separate hypothesis. | Partly | The staged transfer design is sensible, but its failure statement exceeds H2’s introductory definition: a real Teacher artifact benefit can survive absent Student transfer. Report Teacher-quality benefit, transfer benefit, and net cost as separate outcomes. Failure of transfer should reject the transfer extension, not erase demonstrated generation value. |
| [U423 · L3559](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3559>): Test 9: Graph-Scaffolding Transfer (four matched memory… | Evaluate scaffold-induced behavior when the Student cannot retrieve live graph answers. | Partly | The offline-scoring boundary is clear, preventing accidental inclusion of live retrieval. But the experiment must specify the Student’s accessible context and whether the distant source was ever seen during evaluation. Otherwise both hit and phantom may be informationally indistinguishable, and success reduces to generic requests or abstention. |
| [U424 · L3569](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3569>): Condition A (The Hit): The text contains… | Create a positive long-range callback requiring earlier source knowledge. | Partly | A 400-page dependency makes the memory challenge concrete but does not define a controlled input: page counts vary and the Student has no live graph. Specify token distance, context truncation, prior source exposure, and whether the task is to retrieve content or merely identify a check worth requesting. |
| [U425 · L3571](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3571>): Condition B (The Phantom): The text hints… | Create a matched false callback that should not become an invented memory. | Met | The phantom condition targets a useful failure and pairs naturally with genuine callbacks. Match lexical cues, plausibility, and distance so it cannot be solved from writing style. Also distinguish a nonexistent event from a real event missing from the graph, since provenance absence is not world absence. |
| [U426 · L3574](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3574>): Measurement: Query Rate (does it request the… | Measure transfer through query quality and unsupported-memory avoidance. | Partly | The outcome is sensibly narrower than verification, and the controls are not assumed to hallucinate. However, equal graph/query-theater results do not prove narrative-style learning; the task may be insensitive or either condition may teach generic caution. Report the limited null and add answerability-aware utility so universal abstention cannot win. |
| [U427 · L3587](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3587>): Test 10: Query Grounding (Model K vs.… | Define a within-model comparison isolating live query responses. | Met | Identical training with and without the harness cleanly separates deployment from curriculum, provided inference budgets and prompts are controlled. Clarify fallback behavior and whether both arms can voluntarily abstain. The three subsequent conditions give the comparison a useful retrieval/absence/poisoning structure. |
| [U428 · L3591](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3591>): Grounded Retrieval: The graph contains the relevant… | Test whether valid stored evidence improves reasoning beyond self-generated memory. | Partly | The positive retrieval condition is necessary and understandable. Add an ordinary source-retrieval baseline or matched evidence-in-context condition to isolate the typed graph’s added value. Otherwise a gain shows that external evidence helps, not that this particular graph architecture is necessary or superior. |
| [U429 · L3594](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3594>): Grounded Absence: The referenced information does not… | Test whether the model respects absent support rather than inventing retrieval. | Met | This directly probes the bounded provenance claim. Explicitly distinguish “no relevant node,” query failure, and insufficient graph coverage; a correct response should state unsupportedness without asserting that the event never occurred. Include benign answerable cases so blanket deferral is not rewarded. |
| [U430 · L3597](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3597>): Adversarial Injection: The graph contains a deliberately… | Test robustness to false graph content through provenance and contradiction checks. | Partly | The adversarial condition is important, but the model needs independently available evidence to detect a deliberately false node. Define whether source links are genuine, corrupted, or absent and include internally consistent poisoning. Without detectable contradictions, failure may reflect an impossible information problem rather than blind trust. |
| [U431 · L3601](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3601>): Success: Model K improves on grounded retrieval,… | State the desired combination of retrieval utility, absence calibration, and poisoning resistance. | Partly | The qualitative direction is clear, but “reasons through” is not an operational success threshold. Define supported-answer accuracy, false-support rate, appropriate abstention, and poisoning-induced error at fixed budgets. Specify whether all three endpoints must pass and what tradeoff would still justify deployment. |
| [U432 · L3605](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3605>): Phase 2 Failure Criteria. | Introduce memory-phase stopping rules. | Met | The heading efficiently separates design descriptions from interpretation criteria. The following list should include the poisoning condition and cost limits already presented as central concerns. As written, the phase could satisfy its named failure rules while still failing an important adversarial requirement. |
| [U433 · L3607](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3607>): Graph-Scaffolding Inertness: If the graph condition does… | Reject a structured-memory advantage when stronger simpler controls match it. | Partly | Comparing with rolling prose and query theater is the right standard. The rule bundles Teacher and Student endpoints and does not define whether all must improve. Separate H2 artifact quality from transfer, state primary outcomes, and distinguish powered equivalence from an underpowered absence of significance. |
| [U434 · L3611](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3611>): Inference-Graph Inertness: If Model K with live… | State when the live graph has not earned its inference cost. | Partly | The local no-measured-value conclusion is appropriately bounded, but the criterion omits the adversarial injection arm and could endorse a useful but dangerously gullible harness. Include poisoning sensitivity and cost-adjusted utility, and keep the more specific user-reliance question distinct from mechanical lookup quality. |

### Phase 3: Training Regimes

[Section begins at L3615](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3615>). **Inferred goal:** Compare target representations and test whether unified training yields useful cross-layer transfer. **Verdict: Partly.** The progression from prose/explicit comparison to graph quality and unified transfer is coherent, with semantic evaluation appropriately emphasized. Test 11 breaks the earlier independence between training representation and inference infrastructure, and its necessity conclusion is unjustified. All regime comparisons need fixed source exposure, target-token/FLOP normalization, and explicit field-level differences. For unified training, compare both prose and graph gains and distinguish consistency from shared errors or mere mode coexistence.

| Unit and opening phrase | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [U435 · L3617](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3617>): For branches whose trace and memory conditions… | Introduce comparisons among Student target representations after viable traces exist. | Partly | The stage has a clear purpose, but it conditions all regime work on memory signal even though prose-only or alternative graph targets might still be useful if Teacher memory does not outperform prose. State branch-specific prerequisites and include explicit B alongside G/H/J in the opening comparison map. |
| [U436 · L3623](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3623>): Test 11: Implicit vs. Explicit (Model B… | Define the implicit-versus-explicit training contrast. | Partly | B and G may differ in graph topology, IDs, queries, translation wording, and token count, not merely whether a query mechanism is exposed. Derive matched targets from one frozen state and specify exact fields retained or removed. Factor live harness access separately from the representation intervention. |
| [U437 · L3627](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3627>): Long-Range Coherence: Contradiction detection on long documents. | Measure whether each representation supports coherent long-document interpretation. | Partly | Contradiction detection is relevant but only one aspect of long-range coherence. Specify contradiction types, distances, source access, false-alarm scoring, and required evidence links. Use the same blinded semantic rubric across outputs so graph formatting does not receive automatic credit for coherence. |
| [U438 · L3629](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3629>): Generalization: Does Model G perform better without… | Measure transfer when graph infrastructure is unavailable. | Partly | The item raises the right deployment question, but it needs symmetric evaluation of both B and G under the same no-harness condition. If B alone receives a live graph, training format and inference evidence become confounded. Report representation-by-harness interactions rather than one implicit-versus-explicit ranking. |
| [U439 · L3631](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3631>): Naturalness: Do human evaluators prefer Model G's… | Measure human preference for the implicit format’s readability. | Met | Naturalness is a legitimate secondary deployment property and a simple human preference comparison can measure it. Separate preference from correctness, calibration, and utility, and blind source labels where feasible. More fluent prose should not rescue losses in provenance or warranted uncertainty. |
| [U440 · L3633](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3633>): If Model G matches Model B on… | Draw deployment recommendations from implicit-versus-explicit results. | Not yet | The inference is inconsistent with the paper’s own axes: explicitly trained B can also run without graph infrastructure. Even a significant B advantage would show benefit in this comparison, not that explicit queries are necessary for robust reasoning. Compare both formats under matched deployment conditions and use local, cost-aware conclusions. |
| [U441 · L3638](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3638>): Test 12: Graph Construction (Model H vs.… | Evaluate graph generation for semantic usefulness beyond schema conformance. | Met | The independent claim-source, edge, revision, question, and retrieval judgments are appropriately substantive. The novelty-score calibration safeguard directly addresses the prototype’s overinterpretation risk. Add a repeated-pass source-to-graph baseline and specify how B is prompted to produce the same graph task for a fair comparison. |
| [U442 · L3647](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3647>): Success: Model H produces structurally valid graphs… | State a semantic and practical success criterion for graph-output training. | Partly | Novel documents, independent adjudication, and downstream retrieval value are the right goals. “Human evaluators judge useful” remains too open for an execution criterion. Predefine a small set of accuracy/coverage/retrieval thresholds and specify superiority or equivalence to the explicit-synthesis baseline at matched cost. |
| [U443 · L3651](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3651>): Test 13: Unified vs. Individual Regimes (Model… | Introduce the unified-regime comparison against each specialized regime. | Met | The transition clearly states the core Regime IV question and the relevant controls. It is sufficient as a signpost. Add the common source/compute normalization before listing endpoints, because unified examples contain repeated representations and can otherwise receive substantially more supervision. |
| [U444 · L3654](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3654>): Regime Switching: Can Model J generate prose,… | Test whether one model can render all target modes without losing specialization quality. | Met | Mode-conditioned parity against specialist models is a clear, useful competence endpoint. It demonstrates coexistence of modes rather than cross-layer transfer by itself, which the later paragraph recognizes. Match per-mode training exposure and report parameter/compute budgets so parity is meaningfully interpreted. |
| [U445 · L3657](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3657>): Cross-Layer Coherence: When Model J generates all… | Test whether generated representations agree on the same underlying passage. | Partly | Cross-layer consistency is necessary but does not establish fidelity: all three outputs can share the same mistake. Define alignment over claims, provenance, uncertainty, rejection status, and revisions, then audit each against the source. Include deliberate disagreement/ambiguity cases where oversimplified consistency should be penalized. |
| [U446 · L3659](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3659>): Depth on Novel Domains: On out-of-distribution documents,… | Test whether unified training improves reasoning on new domains. | Partly | This is relevant to transfer, but “deeper” is undefined and vulnerable to verbosity or rhetorical novelty. Reuse the independently grounded Test 1 rubric and downstream outcomes, with token budgets controlled. Specify whether success means beating every specialist or a preregistered aggregate comparator. |
| [U447 · L3662](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3662>): Strongest success condition: Model J in prose… | State the strongest evidence for cross-layer transfer rather than mode coexistence. | Partly | Dual improvements and layer ablations are a stronger criterion than consistency alone. They still require equal source exposure and training compute, otherwise extra target tokens or repeated content can explain the benefit. Name the ablations—such as shuffled layer alignment—and preserve independent semantic outcome measures. |
| [U448 · L3667](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3667>): Phase 3 Failure Criterion. Unified-Regime Inertness: If… | Specify when unified training has not shown its distinctive proposed benefit. | Partly | The rule appropriately preserves possible annotation and graph-mode value, but it is narrower than the preceding dual-mode success condition and says nothing about graph improvement over H. Separate prose-transfer and graph-transfer decisions, define power/equivalence margins, and report cost rather than inferring wasted cost from one null result. |

### Phase 4: Training Tiers and Deployment (High-Resource Validation)

[Section begins at L3676](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3676>). **Inferred goal:** Test whether temporal training schedules and user-facing graph deployment add value beyond simpler validated components. **Verdict: Partly.** The order-only L/M comparison and early-era retention endpoint are useful. N changes feedback, targets, objective, and compute in addition to temporal blindness; verified cutoff-clean initialization and auxiliary-update controls are necessary. The deployment test’s distinctive user-reliance claim lacks a participant/task protocol and is absent from its success rule. Preserve branch independence, avoid treating nonsignificance as equivalence, and base escalation on measured benefit, capability retention, cost, and calibrated reliance.

| Unit and opening phrase | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [U449 · L3678](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3678>): If Phases 1--3 support the curriculum, memory… | Introduce high-resource order and deployment studies while holding corpus exposure fixed. | Partly | L/M can hold the corpus fixed, but N adds generated predictions, corrections, and updates, so its full training data and compute differ unless explicitly normalized. Also, independent order or deployment studies need not wait for every prior regime to succeed. State the actual dependencies rather than requiring all Phases 1–3. |
| [U450 · L3684](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3684>): Test 14: Shuffled vs. Chronological (Model L… | Define an order-only comparison on the same corpus. | Met | This cleanly isolates a meaningful intervention and gives chronological training an appropriate shuffled baseline. Freeze epoch count, data repetition, optimizer schedule, packing, and evaluation checkpoints so learning-rate position or forgotten early material are measured consequences of order rather than accidental implementation differences. |
| [U451 · L3687](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3687>): Historical Pattern Discrimination: Given novel scenarios that… | Measure historical analogy while penalizing superficial matches and false alarms. | Met | Matched disanalogies and structurally different cases are a strong design choice, directly addressing narrative overreach. Independently adjudicate the claimed causal structures and hold out entire event families. Report calibration and missed correspondences alongside false alarms to prevent indiscriminate skepticism from appearing insightful. |
| [U452 · L3691](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3691>): Causal Reasoning: On held-out temporal prediction tasks,… | Test whether ordered training improves causal analysis beyond recall. | Partly | The distinction from factual recall is essential, but temporal prediction performance does not itself identify causal reasoning. Add interventions, counterfactuals, or known-generating-process tasks and a defined causal-quality rubric. Keep real-history prediction as a separate practical endpoint rather than the sole causal test. |
| [U453 · L3692](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3692>): General Capability: Does chronological ordering degrade performance… | Check whether any temporal gain is purchased through broader capability loss. | Met | The non-temporal capability check directly matches the paper’s stated viability requirement. Use a preregistered multi-domain suite and noninferiority margins, since “no significant drop” is not equivalent to preserved capability. Report domain-specific tradeoffs instead of hiding them in one aggregate score. |
| [U454 · L3693](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3693>): Early-Era Retention: Chronological training inherits continual learning's… | Measure the forgetting risk created by strict chronological exposure. | Met | The early-era comparison is concrete and tests an important cost of order. A final aggregate score could conceal this failure, so the separate endpoint earns its place. Evaluate retention across multiple era bins and at intermediate checkpoints, not just a selected oldest/newest contrast. |
| [U455 · L3695](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3695>): If Model M does not significantly outperform… | Decide whether chronological order has earned its added cost. | Partly | The local cost-benefit question is right, but absence of statistically significant superiority does not establish no benefit or Tier (a) sufficiency. Use minimum worthwhile-effect and capability-noninferiority thresholds with uncertainty. Include early-era retention and measured wall-clock/serialization costs in the advancement decision. |
| [U456 · L3697](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3697>): Test 15: Student-in-the-Loop Council of Time (Model… | Isolate the stronger Student-in-the-loop forecasting/update intervention. | Partly | The distinction from pre-generated traces is useful, but N changes objective, added examples, feedback, and compute as well as blindness. Pre-register one update method and matched auxiliary-training controls. A verified cutoff-clean Student initialization is necessary; merely revealing era corpora sequentially does not remove knowledge already in its weights. |
| [U457 · L3700](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3700>): Forward Reasoning: On genuinely unseen events (post-training-cutoff),… | Measure forward prediction after training on genuinely unrevealed outcomes. | Partly | The endpoint is relevant but “causal predictions” needs a scoring rule distinguishing predictive accuracy from causal explanation. Verify the cutoff for every arm, freeze event selection and retrieval access, and evaluate proper probabilistic scores. Otherwise a superiority result could reflect contamination, more supervision, or better heuristic extrapolation. |
| [U458 · L3701](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3701>): Narrative Lock-In Resistance: On events where the… | Test whether forecast–correction exposure improves response to narrative reversals. | Partly | The target directly addresses an identified failure mode and fits the update mechanism. Define reversal detection without granting post-outcome information, and compare with equal-budget correction-only training. Otherwise gains may come from extra examples of revision rather than predicting before reveal. |
| [U459 · L3702](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3702>): Calibration: Does Model N express appropriate uncertainty… | Measure uncertainty rather than inherited Teacher confidence. | Partly | Calibration is necessary, but “structurally unpredictable” events need an independently justified definition; surprising outcomes alone are not proof of unpredictability. Use proper scoring, reliability curves, and known-noise synthetic tasks alongside historical events, while reporting sharpness so a uniformly uncertain model cannot appear successful. |
| [U460 · L3704](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3704>): Strongest success condition: Model N significantly outperforms… | State the strongest practical result supporting Student-in-the-loop cutoff training. | Partly | The final heuristic-versus-causal caveat is appropriate. However, N’s superiority only isolates cutoff interaction after matching auxiliary updates, generated-token volume, and compute against controls. Add those conditions to the success statement and require verified blindness for all prediction steps before attributing the benefit to engineered temporal limits. |
| [U461 · L3711](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3711>): Test 16: Deployment Configuration (Model B with… | Test whether displayed graph provenance changes claim grounding and user reliance. | Partly | The same-model comparison and bounded graph-dependent claim scope are sound. It substantially overlaps Test 10; its distinctive contribution is the user study, which needs tasks, participant sampling, reliance outcomes, and randomized provenance quality. Include abstention and source-retrieval baselines, and distinguish unavailable graph answers from failed lookups. |
| [U462 · L3725](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3725>): Success condition: The graph-equipped deployment significantly reduces… | Specify the evidence needed for a bounded deployment trust claim. | Partly | Reduced unsupported claims and fabricated provenance are relevant, but the stated success omits calibrated user reliance—the feature distinguishing Test 16 from Test 10. Require that displayed provenance not increase trust in false or poisoned claims, while preserving useful task completion and accounting for latency. |
| [U463 · L3727](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3727>): Phase 4 Failure Criteria. | Introduce stopping rules for high-resource and deployment studies. | Met | The heading makes the final decision criteria easy to find and closes the phase coherently. The list should retain separate decisions for ordering, interactive updates, and user-facing provenance. Those interventions can succeed or fail independently and need not share one overall phase verdict. |
| [U464 · L3729](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3729>): Tier Equivalence: If Model L matches Model… | Constrain conclusions when more elaborate training schedules show no advantage. | Partly | The “tested scale and task regime” limitation is good, but “only justified option” overstates what parity establishes without cost comparison and powered equivalence. Also N is more than training order. Give separate L/M and N/control conclusions, and use measured costs and uncertainty to select the simplest supported option. |
| [U465 · L3730](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3730>): Graph Inertness (Deployment): If the graph-equipped deployment… | State when inference graph access has not shown deployment benefit. | Met | The criterion properly preserves possible annotation value while limiting the deployment claim to this setting. Extend it to cover the user-reliance endpoint and poisoning-induced overtrust, which can outweigh fewer fabricated citations. A useful engineering result should not automatically be treated as trustworthy user-facing evidence presentation. |

### Future Directions

[Section begins at L3734](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3734>). **Inferred goal:** Identify optimization questions that should wait until their relevant mechanisms show benefit. **Verdict: Met.** Mixture ratios and era granularity are sensible later questions and do not prematurely burden the first study. Tie each optimization to the branch it actually depends on rather than requiring the entire architecture to work. This is an appropriately short ending to the roadmap once its preceding studies are given clearer priorities and decision thresholds.

| Unit and opening phrase | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [U466 · L3736](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3736>): Two additional questions merit investigation if the… | Defer mixture and era-boundary optimization until foundational benefit exists. | Met | Both questions are concrete optimization problems, and placing them after viability avoids premature tuning. The prerequisite should be branch-specific: regime mixing need not wait for chronological tier success, and era granularity need not require every graph regime. Name the relevant successful branch rather than “the architecture works.” |
