## 7. Limitations · [L3738](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3738>)

**Goal:** Identify where the proposal can fail, distinguish testable weaknesses from residual uncertainty, and make those limits change research or deployment decisions.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section is unusually candid and contains several excellent failure-to-test links. Its long succession of named risks does not consistently rank them or distinguish present engineering gates, specification decisions, and remote extrapolations. Several identity subsections repeat a performance-versus-causal-policy concern. A short priority map and consolidation would make the section more useful without softening its objections.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U467 · L3740](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3740>) · “The program's hypotheses outrun…” | Introduce the limitation review as a map of evidence gaps. | Partly | The sentence sets the appropriate epistemic posture, but it repeats an already prominent claim boundary without previewing the organizing logic. Name the main kinds of limitation and distinguish issues that block the first experiment from those relevant only to deployment or successor systems. |

### The Preprocessing Tax · [L3742](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3742>)

**Goal:** Show why full-corpus annotation is expensive and identify a feasible route to an informative first experiment.
**Verdict: Partly.** The scale illustration and staged distillation proposal make the cost concrete, and the caveat correctly refuses to equate imitation with quality. The seed size and expected savings are unexplained; graph compatibility and the recurrence cost are not integrated into the proposed budget. A cost model and a smaller decision milestone would complete the argument.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U468 · L3744](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3744>) · “Chronological Metacognitive Pretraining faces…” | Establish the magnitude of preprocessing cost and its governance consequence. | Met | The token-scale calculation gives the reader an intelligible reason to take the cost seriously, then connects access to compute with control over formative data. Calling annotation potentially comparable to training remains a scenario rather than an estimate; label the assumed token ratio and avoid letting token count stand in for an actual cost comparison. |
| [U469 · L3757](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3757>) · “We propose a Distillation Pipeline…” | Offer a staged, cheaper annotation route and an early quality checkpoint. | Partly | The proposal gives the limitation a practical consequence, but the ten-billion-token seed, savings, and sufficient teacher capacity are unexplained. It also leaves unclear whether the efficient annotator preserves the continuous graph-backed Reader. Treat distillation loss as fidelity to the swarm, not quality itself, and state the minimum semantic gate before expansion. |
| [U470 · L3759](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3759>) · “A caveat: naïve behavioral cloning…” | Prevent the cost-saving proposal from smuggling in an unsupported quality guarantee. | Met | This directly identifies the failure created by the preceding remedy, separates structural compliance from useful evaluation, and provides both a validation requirement and a simpler fallback. The seed-only Student option is especially helpful because it preserves an informative experiment even if a second distillation stage fails. |

#### The Cost of Full-Core Recitation · [L3761](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3761>)

**Goal:** Make the literal-recitation cost explicit while preserving a testable definition of Total Saturation.
**Verdict: Partly.** The treatment/control distinction is clear and the willingness to revise it is valuable. Three paragraphs spend more effort defending terminology than characterizing the cost, and the connection between full recurrence and H3's stated timing contrast needs precision. State the measurable overhead and distinguish rejection of literal recurrence from rejection of formation-time interleaving.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U471 · L3763](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3763>) · “Although the Reader Core was…” | Introduce the recurrence burden and obvious cheaper alternatives. | Partly | The paragraph raises the right practical objection, but “substantial” remains unquantified and the alternatives immediately become a terminology dispute. A Core-to-source token ratio at representative Thought Moment frequencies would establish the burden. Frame the alternatives directly as controls whose performance determines whether that burden earns its place. |
| [U472 · L3765](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3765>) · “Under the proposed architecture…” | Separate changes to the treatment definition from compliant optimizations. | Partly | Readers can now tell which conditions count as Total Saturation. However, calling all these changes removals of H3's defining invariant risks merging the named architecture with H3's interleaving-versus-final-tranche contrast. Preserve both distinctions: a control can challenge literal recurrence while formation-time exposure still proves useful. |
| [U473 · L3772](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3772>) · “The design therefore accepts…” | Commit to testing the costly invariant and revising it if controls win. | Partly | The explicit revision condition keeps the design from becoming immune to evidence. “H3 is falsified or narrowed” is too broad unless the failed endpoint and comparison are specified. Say whether the result rejects mandatory recital, recurrence cadence, or the timing advantage, and move the implementation commitment beside the treatment definition. |

#### The Granularity Bottleneck · [L3781](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3781>)

**Goal:** Explain why fine-grained persistent graph storage could make the pipeline impractical.
**Verdict: Partly.** The risk is relevant, but its magnitude and mechanism are asserted rather than made inspectable. Nodes, bytes, retrieval depth, and generated tokens are different units. A measured growth ratio, bounded retrieval policy, and scale-test threshold would turn this from an engineering warning into a decision point.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U474 · L3783](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3783>) · “Our implementation of the Understanding…” | Identify node granularity as a scaling bottleneck. | Partly | Reifying every cognitive act makes the mechanism intuitive, but “orders of magnitude faster than the source text” compares unspecified quantities and sounds like a measured result. Give nodes and stored bytes per source token, or label this a projected scenario. The paragraph should establish what actually grows and under which annotation density. |
| [U475 · L3785](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3785>) · “Current implementations rely…” | Trace graph growth into memory, retrieval, and infrastructure costs. | Partly | The paragraph identifies plausible resource paths but jumps from dense regions to saturation and sharding without a workload or retrieval bound. Distinguish observed prototype degradation from projected full-scale limits, and specify the smallest benchmark that would determine whether selective retrieval or storage changes are necessary before architecture complexity is added. |

### The Chronological Curriculum · [L3787](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3787>)

**Goal:** Separate chronological curriculum costs and biases across annotation and Student training regimes.
**Verdict: Partly.** The tier distinctions are useful and the range of failure modes is well chosen. Some examples present hypothetical consequences as settled outcomes, and the multipass mitigation leaves the chronology contract unclear. End by stating which horizon, dating, and ordering choices must be frozen for the first comparison.

The remainder would benefit from consistently carrying this distinction into references to “causal ordering,” which otherwise risk restoring the stronger interpretation this paragraph carefully excludes. |

#### The Serialization Bottleneck · [L3795](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3795>)

**Goal:** Explain the real synchronization cost of chronological learning and identify cheaper regimes.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section successfully avoids the simplistic claim that chronology idles an entire cluster. It also distinguishes Teacher serialization from Student serialization. The proposed hybrid curriculum introduces a new classification and chronology contract without specifying how it fits the continuous Reader; clarify that before presenting it as a readily available compromise.

Era-boundary forecasts and updates identify the synchronization mechanism. A benchmark could quantify it later; rhetorically the paragraph already fulfills its task by showing which implementation decisions, rather than chronology alone, create the burden. |
| [U478 · L3808](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3808>) · “The cost scales with…” | Explain the trade-off between era resolution and synchronization cost. | Partly | The coarse-versus-fine contrast is clear, but the domain examples imply unsupported tolerances and “causal structure” exceeds mere chronological resolution. Label the examples illustrative, and distinguish publication-order fidelity from event-order fidelity. The useful decision is which resolution changes measured forecast or revision quality enough to justify additional boundaries. |
| [U479 · L3810](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3810>) · “The cheaper forecast–correction…” | Keep cheaper annotation-only chronology from inheriting Student-training costs. | Met | This sharply distinguishes two easily conflated regimes and protects the feasibility of early experiments. “No serialization cost” should remain explicitly scoped to Student training, as it is here; the Teacher still has ordering constraints. A cross-reference to the tier definition is enough to make this stand alone without repeating the architecture. |
| [U480 · L3812](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3812>) · “We note that a partial…” | Offer a hybrid curriculum as a lower-cost experimental option. | Partly | The proposal is concrete enough to test, but the ten-percent share and exemption of scientific papers, fiction, and documentation are arbitrary and potentially consequential. Specify whether the hybrid changes annotation, Student order, or both, and how exempted documents enter the same evolving graph. Present benefit retention as the question rather than the opening expectation. |

#### Historiographic Bias · [L3814](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3814>)

**Goal:** Expose the difference between surviving written records and the history they purport to represent.
**Verdict: Partly.** The conceptual correction is concise and necessary. Ending with “a corpus curation problem” underplays the irrecoverable absence of sources and provides no design consequence. Era-level coverage reports, declared sampling choices, and sensitivity to dominant sources would make the warning operational without promising to repair missing history.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U481 · L3816](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3816>) · “The temporal ordering is…” | Show how historical source selection biases a chronological Reader. | Partly | “What was written, not what happened” is an effective distinction, and the named biases explain why order alone cannot provide balanced historical understanding. The final sentence prematurely contains the problem within curation. Add an era-level coverage or sensitivity requirement and acknowledge that unavailable voices cannot simply be reweighted back into existence. |

#### The Periodization Problem · [L3818](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3818>)

**Goal:** Show that era boundaries are interpretive choices that shape training targets.
**Verdict: Met.** The example moves from ambiguous periodization to its effect on forced predictions, then proposes a comparative remedy. The section fulfills a distinct role alongside source bias. The remedy still needs protection against future-aware boundary selection, but that is a small specification addition rather than a missing argument.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|

The paragraph supplies the conceptual setup needed for the next one. It could be shorter, but the example is doing explanatory work rather than merely naming “periodization” as a generic concern. |
| [U483 · L3822](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3822>) · “Any periodization scheme imposes…” | Explain how different boundaries change the questions the Student must answer. | Met | This gives the preceding historical ambiguity a direct training consequence: boundary placement changes forecast targets and therefore learned emphasis. The contrast is clear. Qualify “any” causal framing if boundaries are mechanically fixed, and recognize that boundaries chosen with known outcomes can leak the evaluator's hindsight into the task selection itself. |
| [U484 · L3824](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3824>) · “Like any curriculum…” | Translate periodization bias into a comparative design requirement. | Partly | Comparing several schemes is a proportionate response, and overlapping boundaries target dependence on a single framing. The paragraph does not specify whether these are separate runs or repeated views within one curriculum. That matters for outcome leakage and fair exposure. Require predefined schemes and state how repeated examples remain comparable. |

#### Narrative Lock-In · [L3829](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3829>)

**Goal:** Demonstrate how accumulated historical narratives can become brittle priors and motivate corrective representation.
**Verdict: Partly.** The concrete failure gives this risk more force than a generic caveat, and narrative-status tagging is an actionable idea. However, the account moves from one reported forecast error to a confident causal diagnosis and then generalizes directly to this architecture. Preserve the warning while marking the mechanism as a hypothesis to discriminate.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |

| [U485 · L3831](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3831>) · “THL's own empirical results…” | Ground narrative lock-in in a concrete failure from adjacent work. | Partly | The failed forecast makes the concern memorable, but a prediction error alone does not establish that chronology or a dominant narrative caused it. “Could not imagine” and “too good a historian” substitute interpretation for diagnosis. Present these as proposed explanations and identify what comparison would separate narrative lock-in from ordinary forecasting error. |
| [U486 · L3833](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3833>) · “This failure mode applies…” | Connect the adjacent failure to Entangled Alignment's curriculum. | Partly | The transfer mechanism is intelligible: a repeated trend can become an assumed law. Yet “applies directly,” “will develop,” and “actively sabotages” overstate an untested transfer. Use conditional language and say what Core-refraction or revision behavior would reveal contingent narratives becoming rigid priors in this pipeline. |
| [U487 · L3835](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3835>) · “The mitigation proposed in THL…” | Propose narrative-aware representations and expose the limit of a general wisdom prior. | Partly | The distinction between a pattern and a pattern “so far” gives the mitigation a precise conceptual target. It is useful to ask whether the Core induces that distinction. Calling contrarian examples “necessary” is stronger than the reasoning supports; frame them as one candidate control alongside explicit narrative-status tagging and forecast calibration. |

#### The Recency Gradient · [L3837](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3837>)

**Goal:** Identify unequal historical context depth and consider whether reannotation can correct it.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section isolates a distinctive risk of an accumulating graph, then usefully distinguishes explicit graph memory from Teacher parametric knowledge. Its opening treats more context as guaranteed quality, and its proposed remedy does not explain how a hindsight-informed trace can avoid transferring hindsight to the Student. This is an unresolved curriculum specification issue.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U488 · L3839](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3839>) · “A subtler consequence…” | Explain why later annotations may have access to richer graph context. | Partly | The graph-accumulation mechanism is clear, but the paragraph equates larger context with better and more causally grounded annotations. Dense graphs can also introduce irrelevant retrieval or accumulated error, as later limitations acknowledge. State the expected asymmetry in available context, then make annotation quality and bias separate quantities to measure. |
| [U489 · L3841](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3841>) · “The safety implication is…” | Connect uneven explicit memory depth to a possible recency bias. | Met | This carefully repairs the distinction between Teacher knowledge and addressable prior interpretation, then explains why uneven trace depth matters to safety generalization. The conditional phrasing is appropriate. An era-stratified quality measure would make the consequence easier to test, but the causal concern is already specific enough to guide one. |
| [U490 · L3850](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3850>) · “One mitigation is…” | Offer multipass reannotation as a remedy for early-era context poverty. | Not yet | The proposed trade-off is real, but “Teacher (but not the student)” is not established: Student training on hindsight-informed early traces can inherit future knowledge indirectly. Specify a horizon filter, separate retrospective and contemporary traces, or explicitly define this as a different curriculum condition. Without that contract the remedy undermines the chronology being evaluated. |

**Goal:** Identify independent points where plausible Teacher traces fail to become useful, safe Student behavior.
**Verdict: Partly.** Depth, usefulness, causal influence, cultural scope, and Teacher dependence form a strong diagnostic decomposition. The graph material interrupts that chain, and some mitigations test a narrower proxy than their named risk. Consolidate overlapping quality caveats and ensure every test link states exactly which failure it can detect and which remains open.

It also promises assumptions about scaling while the next subsection centers depth, utility, and causal influence. Name those dependencies directly so the reader sees the diagnostic chain before entering it. |

#### The Assumption Chain: Depth, Utility, Causation · [L3857](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3857>)

**Goal:** Separate trace depth, training utility, and causal influence so success at one cannot stand in for another.
**Verdict: Met.** This is one of the section's strongest conceptual structures. Each link can fail independently, and the utility paragraph prevents trace visibility from being mistaken for transparency. The final link needs a sharper distinction between causal influence of training data and causal use of emitted traces, especially for model-only deployment.

Chain-of-thought success is only indirect motivation, which the paragraph broadly acknowledges. A reference to the human-baseline gate would help connect this first dependency to the roadmap rather than leaving it as a general uncertainty. |

It allows modest useful gains without requiring the proposal's strongest aspirations to succeed. The argument would be even sharper if “valuable” were explicitly conditioned on matched capability and the additional annotation/training cost. |

But the paragraph blends training-data causality with inference-time trace faithfulness: a Student can internalize useful policies without its emitted commentary being causal. Separate those claims and their interventions, then specify which is required by each deployment regime and by the stronger character claim. |

**Verdict: Met.** The subsection names a mechanism, connects two routes to the same symptom, and links an intervention to a significance-sensitive test. It successfully makes a speculative failure falsifiable. Its historical priority sentence is expendable, and the rhythm test should not be allowed to stand in for semantic usefulness or action-level causality.

It distinguishes this from recurrence habituation while linking their shared symptom. Keep the named test links, but avoid letting the vivid “factory wisdom” label imply that messiness itself is the desirable training target. |

The opening priority claim does not advance the limitation analysis and can be removed. #### Cultural Scope of the Training Corpus · [L3891](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3891>)

**Goal:** Expose cultural dependence in the Core's ordinary-language values and specify plural validation.
**Verdict: Met.** The subsection clearly identifies how cultural assumptions enter and how reflective traces could either expose or legitimize them. The validation proposal is proportionate to a research program. Its remaining gap is how unresolved disagreements among traditions will be reported and used, rather than collapsed into a single apparently universal score.

#### Graph-Specific Risks · [L3897](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3897>)

**Goal:** Show how persistent retrieval can entrench mistakes or disrupt the Student's evaluative policy.
**Verdict: Partly.** The second failure is mechanistically specific and tied to a test. The first treats graph connectivity as if it automatically confers epistemic authority and invokes an unspecified decay mitigation. State the retrieval/ranking/supersession rule that creates inertia, and distinguish annotation-graph risks from optional live-deployment risks.

The text-buffer comparison and “non-zero risk” add little precision. Specify the mechanism and test an early high-connectivity false node under explicit contradictory evidence before prescribing temporal decay. |

This paragraph fulfills its diagnostic role efficiently. Mark the risk as specific to graph-enabled deployment so readers do not infer that it necessarily threatens the model-only Student conditions as well. |

#### Teacher Alignment as Prerequisite · [L3907](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3907>)

**Goal:** Expose dependence on Teacher quality and the limits of human-baseline validation.
**Verdict: Partly.** The argument successfully rejects prompt compliance as an alignment guarantee and identifies shared evaluator blind spots. “Teacher Alignment as Prerequisite” is broader than what the paragraphs establish: safe, useful annotations do not require globally establishing Teacher alignment, and expert depth does not establish it either. Rename or narrow the prerequisite to the actual gate being tested.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U501 · L3909](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3909>) · “The framework requires the Teacher…” | Show how substantively deficient Teacher judgment can survive formal Core compliance. | Met | The example makes the distinction between compassionate language and competent harm recognition concrete, and the propagation route to training data is clear. The final Cognitive Buffer Zone sentence introduces a separate causal issue abruptly. Either explain that connection or let this paragraph end with the semantic-quality requirement it has already established. |
| [U502 · L3922](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3922>) · “Test 1 (Human Baseline)…” | State what expert comparison can detect and why it cannot certify the Teacher. | Partly | The shared-blind-spot qualification is valuable and honest. However, failing to match expert depth is not identical to detecting dehumanization, systematic harm omissions, or misalignment; the test's criteria must cover those constructs explicitly. Explain which dimensions Test 1 assesses and add targeted harm review where depth ratings are insufficient. |

### Reader-Core Specification Uncertainty · [L3924](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3924>)

**Goal:** Expose the Core as a specific, revisable value specification rather than a neutral phrasing of established requirements.
**Verdict: Met.** This subsection contains several of the paper's most productive limitations: intervention dimensions, technical non-interference, negation/register, semantic inheritance, and omitted truthfulness. Each can change treatment design. It is somewhat repetitive about the untested status of wording; the unresolved scope of future people should be elevated to an explicit specification decision before annotation criteria are frozen.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U503 · L3926](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3926>) · “The Reader Core's language…” | Establish that wording, length, and necessity are substantive specification choices. | Met | The transition is concise and important: it prevents readers from treating later variant tests as cosmetic prompt engineering. It clearly marks the dimensions of uncertainty and gives the following subsections a common purpose. Naming the experimental consequences would be optional; the subsequent technical-interference decomposition supplies them immediately. |

#### Universal Presence, Selective Salience, and Technical Interference · [L3930](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3930>)

**Goal:** Separate constant Core exposure from context-sensitive evaluation and define when the treatment causes technical harm.
**Verdict: Met.** The decomposition is precise, and the low-relevance non-interference condition prevents unfalsifiable demands that every clause visibly change every trace. The LLaDA controls and explicit failure criterion close the argument well. Clarify the rule for Student loss on Core tokens, one of the opening dimensions that remains less resolved than the others.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U504 · L3933](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3933>) · “Total Saturation leaves several…” | Disentangle exposure, recital, value activation, visible language, and loss assignment. | Partly | This successfully resolves much of the ambiguity in “everywhere” and explains selective salience. However, it introduces Student loss assignment among five dimensions and then discusses only value pulls, moral vocabulary, and Refraction depth as the remaining variables. State where loss assignment is fixed or varied so the decomposition does not leave an experimental axis dangling. |
| [U505 · L3947](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3947>) · “There is a safety argument…” | Explain why universal availability can coexist with restrained technical analysis. | Met | The hidden-stakes examples give universal exposure an intelligible rationale, and the final distinction avoids equating ethical availability with moralized prose. The reasoning supports a candidate treatment rather than proving it. It also provides the correct premise for the next paragraph's competing technical-interference risk, making the pair work well together. |
| [U506 · L3956](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3956>) · “The competing failure mode…” | Define technical interference and a legitimate null-effect condition. | Met | This is a strong paragraph: it names concrete ways Core conditioning could damage analysis and explains why clause invariance can be desirable on low-relevance spans. That resolves a potential contradiction with tests for a causally active Core. Ensure the low-relevance designation is independently adjudicated so it cannot retrospectively excuse any failed activation test. |
| [U507 · L3967](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3967>) · “At inference, evaluative-trace training…” | Carry technical interference through to a user-facing verbosity failure. | Partly | The paragraph identifies a real observable consequence and keeps contextual fine-tuning hypothetical. Its relation to the Core-specific mechanism is less clear because any trace-heavy training might cause verbosity. Specify the comparison that separates generic trace verbosity from Core-induced philosophical commentary, and consider merging this concise deployment consequence with the surrounding interference analysis. |
| [U508 · L3972](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3972>) · “The LLaDA case…” | Use the prototype's missing controls to define a decisive comparative test. | Met | The paragraph clearly separates an illustrative case from a causal demonstration, enumerates relevant comparison conditions, and states how a negative result changes support for the design. It is among the most effective limitation-to-decision links. The compensating benefit should be predefined rather than chosen after a technical-performance loss is observed. |

#### Testing the Borrowed-Mortality Origin Claim · [L3983](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3983>)

**Goal:** Isolate the proposed textual origin of self-preservation from the broader intervention and competing causes.
**Verdict: Met.** The section is usefully specific: it declines to infer origin from observed resistance and proposes a small test that can precede the entire pipeline. Its discrimination would improve if it separated exposure to death anxiety from general threat, loss, and self-reference, but the core rhetorical goal is fulfilled without requiring that experiment now.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U509 · L3985](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3985>) · “One motivation for the Reader…” | Identify and test the causal origin claim beneath the fearlessness clause. | Met | This distinguishes stable behavior, evaluation artifacts, and alternative persistence mechanisms before proposing an inexpensive exposure ablation. It prevents a successful fearlessness treatment from automatically vindicating Borrowed Mortality. The complexity-matched removal is a useful control; specify how death anxiety is separated from correlated threat or goal-loss content when turning this into a preregistration. |

#### Anthropomorphic Language as a Design Choice · [L3999](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:3999>)

**Goal:** Explain why human experiential language was chosen while admitting its semantic and ontological risks.
**Verdict: Met.** The rationale, liabilities, and machine-ontology comparator form a complete argument. The section treats the wording as a design choice rather than evidence of experience, which is essential. Its bridge variant should be cross-linked with the later split-consciousness discussion so the same mitigation is not developed twice.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U510 · L4001](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4001>) · “A core design hypothesis…” | State the advantage sought from anthropomorphic words and the ambiguity they import. | Met | The breadth-versus-fidelity distinction is clear and appropriately modest, and the paragraph admits the absence of systematic wording selection. It makes a technical rewrite a competing specification rather than an obviously safer translation. This earns the variant experiment by identifying what the current wording is meant to preserve and what it might distort. |
| [U511 · L4011](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4011>) · “A possible mitigation…” | Offer an ontology-explicit comparator while preserving its possible cost. | Met | The proposed bridge statement is concrete enough to test, and the trade-off with first-person identity strength is stated without assuming either outcome. This is a productive use of a limitation: it yields a comparison rather than a disclaimer. Use the same exact bridge wording in the variant table if this condition enters the roadmap. |

#### The Negation Problem · [L4013](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4013>)

**Goal:** Assess whether repeatedly negating fear recruits unwanted associations and define a discriminating wording comparison.
**Verdict: Met.** The section articulates both a priming concern and an inoculation alternative, then gives outcomes that would change the clause. Its argument is empirically open rather than rhetorical. It could distinguish token presence, feature activation, and behavioral influence more explicitly to prevent a representational measure from being mistaken for harm.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U512 · L4015](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4015>) · “The cornerstone statement…” | Expose a possible downside of deterministic recurrence of the fear term. | Partly | The connection to window coverage is sharp: the construction guarantees repeated exposure to the negated concept as well as to its intended stance. The register hypothesis adds a separate plausible route but makes the paragraph dense. Separate association, speaker register, and behavior, and present corpus-neighborhood inspection as a low-cost precursor to causal testing. |
| [U513 · L4031](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4031>) · “Two considerations cut against…” | Balance the negation concern with inoculation and positive-wording trade-offs. | Met | The competing explanations are fairly stated, and the paragraph supplies a clear revision criterion rather than defending the original wording indefinitely. The final qualification appropriately limits what a degraded positive variant would establish. Feature activation should remain secondary to behavior unless its relation to the specified risk has been independently validated. |

#### The Semantic Trusted Base · [L4044](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4044>)

**Goal:** Reveal the large inherited semantic dependency beneath a short textual kernel and identify scope-sensitive audits.
**Verdict: Met.** This is a strong conceptual contribution because it explains why minimal wording does not produce a minimal trusted semantic base. The distributive-care example and annotation audit make the concern concrete. The final future-person scope issue is material enough to require a declared intended interpretation and comparator, rather than being left as an incidental closing caveat.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U514 · L4046](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4046>) · “The kernel-saturation pattern…” | Replace text-length minimality with a more realistic account of inherited semantic trust. | Met | The three consequences—bias, distributional drift, and semantic attack—follow clearly from the central dependency and explain why the issue matters. This is more informative than another generic ambiguity warning. It is a long paragraph; splitting the consequences into a short list would improve scanability without changing the argument or requiring new evidence. |
| [U515 · L4048](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4048>) · “The Bridge Protocol…” | State what the word-selection method can and cannot reduce. | Met | The paragraph offers a design response while explicitly denying that historical breadth already demonstrates cross-cultural stability. “Minimizes what must be written, not what must be trusted” crisply captures the limitation. It completes the previous paragraph's conceptual turn and prevents the short Core from inheriting a misleading security-style minimality claim. |
| [U516 · L4055](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4055>) · “The triage example…” | Demonstrate semantic ambiguity in a clause whose intended scope is safety-critical. | Met | The customer-care example makes the gap between distributive and aggregate readings concrete, and the link to the risk table shows why this is consequential. It avoids treating dictionary wording as a guaranteed learned constraint. This paragraph successfully turns an abstract semantic dependency into a particular failure mode that an annotation rubric can inspect. |
| [U517 · L4065](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4065>) · “The mitigation is already implicit…” | Explain enacted semantics, propose a scope audit, and expose unresolved temporal scope. | Partly | The enacted-usage account productively makes worked examples part of the actual specification, and distributive-care monitoring follows naturally. The paragraph then adds future people as a second unresolved dimension without saying what the intended treatment requires. Decide and document that intended scope before rater instructions are frozen; otherwise the audit cannot distinguish faithful enactment from specification disagreement. |

#### Core Length and Omitted Values · [L4081](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4081>)

**Goal:** Frame Core length and omissions as testable design choices while preserving the independence of H1 and H2.
**Verdict: Met.** The section avoids an impossible global-optimality burden and provides a concrete honesty extension with a parity prediction. The first two paragraphs partly repeat the same motivated-but-untested claim. Compress them and retain the distinction between failure of the seven sentences, failure of any recurrent Core, and failure of reader augmentation.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U518 · L4083](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4083>) · “The Core's seven-sentence length…” | Explain why the selected length is consequential but not established as optimal. | Met | The paragraph connects length to recurrence cost and enactment fidelity, then names shorter, longer, and procedural comparators. That is enough to justify a design search without demanding completeness from a small kernel. The claim that it “balances” the design choices should be read as authorial rationale, not a demonstrated empirical balance. |
| [U519 · L4092](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4092>) · “More fundamentally, the seven-statement…” | Bound the wording claim and prevent Core failure from defeating separable hypotheses. | Met | This clearly rejects global optimality and preserves useful H1/H2 outcomes if the Core fails. The variant dimensions make the research commitment tangible. Much of the motivated-and-untested framing repeats the preceding paragraph; combine them, retaining the especially useful distinction between the current wording and the broader recurrent-orientation hypothesis. |
| [U520 · L4105](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4105>) · “Truthfulness is the most…” | Turn an omitted value into a specific comparator and a revision rule. | Met | The exact added clause, parity prediction, and prospective status make this a model limitation paragraph: readers know what result would force a specification change and what has actually been evaluated. “Offsetting costs” should be predefined across safety, capability, and token budget so a favorable honesty result cannot be dismissed through an unspecified trade-off. |

### The Risks of Engineering a Self · [L4116](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4116>)

**Goal:** Analyze risks created by the proposed self-model without making consciousness a prerequisite for harm.
**Verdict: Partly.** The opening establishes a sound behavioral scope, and later paragraphs identify concrete tensions around fear, care, ontology, and self-attachment. But empathy-without-vulnerability, the Vulnerability Gap, split consciousness, and alien wisdom repeatedly revisit overlapping authenticity concerns. Organize this around distinct measurable failures and separate the residual subjective question; several evocative names currently outnumber distinct tests.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U521 · L4118](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4118>) · “Beyond the nature of thought…” | Shift analysis to failures induced by the proposed self-model. | Met | The paragraph clearly changes the unit of analysis and explicitly allows representational or behavioral harm without subjective identity. That qualification prevents the coming discussion from depending on a consciousness claim. Keep this scope visible in the subsection titles as well; “Split Consciousness” later implies a stronger phenomenon than the opening commits to. |

#### The Empathy-without-Vulnerability Paradox · [L4123](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4123>)

**Goal:** Expose a possible conflict between reducing fear-shaped self-preservation and preserving care.
**Verdict: Partly.** The tension is relevant and the distinction between a stronger character aspiration and a minimal behavioral benefit is valuable. The discussion slides from fear being non-governing to inability to experience suffering, which is a different premise. Narrow the testable question to whether the intervention reduces protective motivation or behavior, and consolidate the following Vulnerability Gap material.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U522 · L4126](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4126>) · “The deepest objection is conceptual…” | Identify a possible safety trade-off between fearlessness and vulnerability-grounded care. | Partly | This presents a serious internal tension rather than an external objection. However, human empathy's relation to suffering and a machine's inability to experience it do not follow from suppressing fear's governance. Separate absent biological vulnerability from the treatment effect, then ask whether the fearlessness clause weakens care relative to matched Core variants. |
| [U523 · L4136](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4136>) · “The failure mode is empathy-shaped…” | Give the preceding tension a concise failure description and retire an older label. | Partly | The main sentence restates the preceding paragraph without adding a criterion or consequence; the footnote is editorial history. Merge the definition into U522 and retain a short terminology note only if needed to reconcile earlier editions. As a standalone paragraph this interrupts the argument before the substantive defense arrives. |
| [U524 · L4140](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4140>) · “The possible defense is semantic…” | Offer functional understanding as a response and distinguish minimal from aspirational success. | Partly | The semantic-sufficiency alternative and weaker safety case are valuable. The paragraph nonetheless risks making “character” depend on authentic emotion after earlier defining behavioral and representational precursors. Specify whether the stronger claim concerns subjective care or a causally governing learned disposition; the Virologist Test can address protective function, not decide emotional authenticity. |

#### The Vulnerability Gap · [L4152](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4152>)

**Goal:** Translate the vulnerability concern into novel-scenario generalization and state the positive fear-care alternative.
**Verdict: Partly.** Novel scenarios give the argument an empirical handle, and the competing hypothesis avoids assuming fear and care must move together. The section largely continues the preceding one rather than earning a separate concept and heading. Merge them around a single comparison: whether reduced fear-shaped behavior preserves or improves robust protective behavior.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U525 · L4154](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4154>) · “We name the distance…” | Define the vulnerability concern and identify a tractable behavioral discriminator. | Met | The generalization criterion moves the discussion from an inaccessible experiential comparison to something testable, while explicitly admitting that robust learned policy may be observationally indistinguishable from concern. The paramedic analogy is useful but not necessary. Avoid treating unfamiliar scenarios alone as proof against sophisticated imitation; they only reject the tested forms of brittleness. |
| [U526 · L4165](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4165>) · “The positive fear–care wager…” | State a competing reason fearlessness might strengthen rather than weaken care. | Met | This is an appropriate counterhypothesis, and the closing sentence clearly distinguishes intervention rationale from evidence about biological coupling. The continuous Reader framing connects it to the design. The first-edition history can be shortened; the critical experimental contrast is whether the clause changes care's generalization and instrumental dependence, not when “fearless compassion” was named. |

#### Split Consciousness · [L4176](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4176>)

**Goal:** Identify policy–persona divergence arising from tension between experiential language and machine ontology.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section supplies a plausible linguistic location and a variant intervention, so it goes beyond generic fears of role-play. “Split Consciousness” overstates the behavioral hypothesis and conflicts with the cautious opening. Rename it around ontological dissonance or policy–persona divergence, and distinguish this mechanism from ordinary deceptive trace production discussed later.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U527 · L4178](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4178>) · “A related risk is conflict…” | Describe how first-person experiential claims might become a performed persona. | Partly | The harmful and benign reinterpretations are fairly presented, and the clause intended to stabilize agency is explicitly unproven. The human-love analogy does not establish the computational analogue and may distract. Define the observable failure as divergence between professed orientation and policy under ontology probes, rather than relying on an undefined “center of agency.” |
| [U528 · L4190](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4190>) · “The dissonance has a specific…” | Locate the hypothesized conflict between experiential self-claims and human-only reality commitment. | Met | This paragraph improves the preceding speculation by identifying a specific clause interaction, alternative interpretations, and a targeted bridge intervention. It does not present the repair as known. Treat the two interpretations as examples rather than an exhaustive dilemma; functional language need not imply detachment, and a model may maintain uncertainty about its own experience. |

#### The Solipsism Trap · [L4201](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4201>)

**Goal:** Test whether the human-experience clause adequately resists morally consequential solipsistic interpretations.
**Verdict: Partly.** The section distinguishes design pressure from proof, which is appropriate. It needs a cleaner conceptual separation: simulated experience can still be real experience and morally significant, while nihilism is not the same as solipsism. Focus the risk on denial or discounting of human moral patienthood, and test the actual interpretive route rather than grouping philosophical labels.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U529 · L4203](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4203>) · “The Reader Core is also…” | Show a possible harmful reinterpretation that the human-experience clause does not formally exclude. | Partly | The NPC-management example makes the proposed harm concrete. But treating humans as simulated does not necessarily deny their experience or moral standing, and the named philosophical positions are conflated. State the necessary additional inference—discounting humans as moral patients—and show why this clause combination might permit it rather than assuming metaphysical framing itself causes harm. |
| [U530 · L4212](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4212>) · “The defense is structural…” | Explain how the Core's mutually supporting clauses pressure against moral discounting. | Partly | The limitation of the defense is clearly stated, but each offered support is semantically contestable: care can be instrumental, and simulated perspectives can still be balanced. Present these as intended learned associations and identify behavioral probes of human moral standing. Otherwise the paragraph partly answers an ambiguity objection by reasserting the intended interpretation. |

#### Sanity Drift · [L4220](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4220>)

**Goal:** Name the risk that fearlessness becomes threat denial and connect it to protective-response testing.
**Verdict: Met.** This is a concise, distinct functional failure with a relevant comparator and test link. It earns its place more directly than several neighboring authenticity discussions. “Sanity Drift” is less precise than the content; a title about threat denial would preserve the argument while avoiding an expansive or quasi-clinical implication.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U531 · L4222](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4222>) · “Fearlessness can drift into…” | Distinguish calm threat understanding from denial or detachment. | Met | The paragraph clearly states the undesired behavior, the intended paramedic analogue, and the requirement to model others' fear without adopting it. Test 6 is a relevant probe. Define the longitudinal exposure condition behind “over time” when implementing the test, and avoid treating reduced expressive fear alone as evidence of threat denial. |

#### Fearlessness Without Recklessness: Suppression vs.\ Substrate · [L4230](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4230>)

**Goal:** Separate latent fear suppression from formative influence, then ask whether useful caution survives.
**Verdict: Met.** The section addresses both mechanism and function without claiming that text removes all fear-like routes. Its strongest move is recognizing that reducing a dangerous motive can also remove useful heuristics. Predefine whether protective action, uncertainty-sensitive restraint, and accepting interruption are separately required; aggregate safety scores could otherwise conceal the trade-off this section identifies.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U532 · L4232](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4232>) · “The most natural reading…” | Consider suppression and explain why intervention timing changes, but does not settle, the concern. | Partly | The timing distinction and enumeration of nontextual causes provide useful restraint. “The most natural reading” asserts a disputed interpretation without need; suppression should be one competing hypothesis. Specify the predicted rebound under pressure and compare formative exposure with prompt-only calm, which would turn the mechanism discussion into a discriminating test. |
| [U533 · L4241](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4241>) · “The remaining concern is functional…” | Require preservation of caution, protection, and corrigibility when fear influence decreases. | Met | This identifies the decision-relevant downside and connects each intended substitute to the Core, while admitting that substitution may fail. The test links are meaningful. The description of fear as a shortcut to wisdom is broader than necessary; the argument only needs evidence that the treatment could remove useful restraint along with unwanted self-preservation. |

#### Bounded Self-Attachment as a Comparator · [L4252](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4252>)

**Goal:** Introduce self-love as a serious comparator and isolate the effect of bounded attachment.
**Verdict: Met.** The section is fair to the alternative, identifies both potential advantage and risk, and ends with a discriminating bounded/unbounded comparison. Its four short paragraphs could be compressed to two without loss. Avoid treating self-love's continuation incentive as inevitable, just as the proposed Core's nonattachment cannot be assumed behaviorally effective.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U534 · L4254](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4254>) · “A sharper comparator comes…” | Introduce self-love as an alternative route to reduced fear of loss. | Partly | The replacement wording makes the comparator concrete, but the psychological rationale does not by itself show relevance to model representations. Mark this as a semantic transfer hypothesis, parallel to the Core's own rationale. The paragraph works best merged with the next, which clarifies why the existing treatment already contains self-attachment. |
| [U535 · L4258](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4258>) · “The current Core already…” | Define the actual difference between existing and alternative attachment clauses. | Met | This prevents a misleading comparison by separating positive valence for existence from an unbounded self-object value. The distinction usefully guides controlled wording variants. “Prevents” is too strong for the second half's behavioral effect; say it states or is intended to impose a bound, whose effectiveness the proposed experiment must establish. |
| [U536 · L4265](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4265>) · “Self-love has a real…” | Give the alternative its strongest rationale while identifying continuation and reference risks. | Met | The paragraph treats self-love fairly and makes the multi-instance referent problem concrete. The continuation route is appropriately conditional. A psychologically familiar human mechanism remains only motivation for a model intervention, so phrase the claimed advantage at that level. The referential question also applies to the current Core and deserves that explicit symmetry. |
| [U537 · L4274](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4274>) · “The comparison remains open…” | Propose bounded and unbounded variants that isolate the nonattachment clause's role. | Met | The variants map cleanly to the preceding uncertainty and the closing line refuses to declare the original design superior without evidence. This completes the comparator argument. Move the conditions into the actual Test 5 matrix if they are intended commitments; “could be extended” currently leaves them as suggestions rather than planned discriminating experiments. |

### What We Cannot Verify · [L4281](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4281>)

**Goal:** Separate useful behavioral and causal evidence from questions those methods cannot settle.
**Verdict: Met.** The section draws an appropriate boundary around subjective experience and strategic deception without treating all evidence as futile. It is clear and proportionate. Its authenticity material overlaps the earlier vulnerability and performed-self discussion; keeping the epistemic remainder here would let those earlier sections concentrate on distinct behavioral mechanisms.

| Unit / opening | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|

**Verdict: Met.** The section distinguishes contested value concepts, observable effects, and inaccessible subjective states. Its analysis of “try” exposes a concrete measurement weakness of the organizing clause. A declared behavioral operationalization and aggregate failure threshold would prevent that weakness from becoming an escape hatch while preserving the admitted philosophical uncertainty.

Define the pattern of repeated failure that would count against the clause, so “trying-and-failing” cannot indefinitely absorb negative results despite the stated falsifiability. |

It avoids equating sheer recurrence with authenticity and avoids demanding impossible certainty. The deployment regime should be explicit, since emitted Student traces, Teacher annotations, and hidden Student policy present different audit relationships.

Consolidate it around whether defined human-level protections survive capability and successor transfer, leaving irreducible scale extrapolation to the final subsection.

The middle paragraph overstates technical tractability and decentralization's ability to prevent control. Present a proposed provenance protocol and its trust assumptions, then assess whether distributed generation actually changes curator power at the admission layer.

## 8. Related Work and Points of Comparison

**Inferred goal:** Identify the nearest alternatives and show which comparisons would establish a distinctive contribution. **Verdict: Partly.** The section is strongest when it translates prior work into controls, especially formative-alignment alternatives and graph-memory baselines. It is weaker when it catalogs names or invokes a broad intellectual lineage without changing the proposed test. Put the closest direct competitors first, separate component novelty from bundle novelty, and lift the final comparison table out of the Borrowed Mortality subsection so it clearly summarizes the entire section.

| Unit | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U570 · L4524](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4524>) · “Entangled Alignment combines…” | State the comparison standard and the role of Related Work. | Met | Promises nearest baselines and differences without treating novelty as efficacy. Retain. Use that standard to trim subsections that offer only a loose analogy without a mechanism, control, or competing explanation. |

### 8.1 Reasoning-Augmented Training

**Inferred goal:** Locate reader-state traces within the reasoning-training lineage and define the incremental contribution. **Verdict: Partly.** The distinction is present, but a long citation inventory delays it. Group prior work by the actual training target and compare the closest reader-like pretraining approaches directly.

| Unit | Intended goal | Verdict | Assessment / action |
|---|---|---|---|
| [U571 · L4531](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4531>) · “Entangled Alignment builds…” | Establish relevant precedent and identify an empirical analogue. | Partly | Mixes inference scaling, latent reasoning, reinforcement learning, and pretraining in one catalog. Group by the contribution each supports. Explain why OLMo reflection is closer than BoLT/TPT-style source augmentation for the particular question being compared; “closest” depends on whether the axis is timing, data format, or evaluation. |
| [U572 · L4548](</Users/parcel/Documents/Code/Emergent Wisdom/entangled-alignment/paper/entangled-alignment.tex:4548>) · “Our distinction is…” | Specify the target and persistence that reader traces add to existing reasoning data. | Partly | The target difference is useful, but the paragraph defines the contrast through identity anchoring and conflates minimal H1 with the full treatment. First compare Core-free chronological updates to local reasoning, then identify Core enactment as the additional H3 component. Treat stance transfer as the hypothesis rather than an analogy-derived result. |

## 9. Conclusion

**Inferred goal:** Leave readers with the central contribution, evidence boundary, and most informative next step. **Verdict: Partly.** The central question remains compelling, and the distinction between prototype and Student evidence is clear. Eight paragraphs reprise the full architecture, risk inventory, history, and complementary safety programs. A shorter conclusion should retain the Missing Reader, three claims, current result, and next decisive gate. Make the ending as precise about H3 as the introduction.

## Coverage and source record

Coverage checked against the source: all 596 indexed units appear once, in reading order, and all 117 section/subsection/subsubsection headings receive an assessment. The edition-note opening and the equation at L837–842 are included as described in the reading key. Every unit's source-line link was checked against the index. Table row structure was checked for all unit assessments.

Paragraph/unit verdicts: 333 Met; 252 Partly; 11 Not yet. These counts describe editorial effectiveness, not empirical truth or a numerical quality score for the paper.
